                                                  पाटीगिणतम् --- चतुर्िंवशितपिरकमार्िण

सं किलतं व्यवकिलतं गुणनं भाजनं तथा
वगार्े वगर्मूलं घनो घनमूलं पञ्चमी त्रयः ॥११७॥
पञ्च िवशे षाः पिरकमार्ण्ये तािन गिणतं स्मृतम्
प्रकीर्िततान्याचायर्ै रेतै ः सवर्ं गिणतं िसध्यित ॥११८॥
रूपै कदे शसं युक्ता व्यक्तरािशरुदाहृता सा
अव्यक्ता या रािशयुर्क्ता सा रूपै कदे शेन ॥११९॥
      वा. भा.---सं किलतं योगः व्यवकिलतं शोधनं गुणनं गुणकारः
      भाजनं हरणं वगर्ः समतुल्यद्वयं वगर्मूलं समतुल्यप्रमाणम् घनः
      सममूलकस्त्रयः घनमूलं तत्प्रमाणं । एतािन पिरकमार्िण गिणतं स्मृतम्
      प्रकीर्िततान्याचायर्ै ः एतै ः सवर्ं गिणतं िसध्यित ॥११७--११९॥
      यो राशे िस्त्रगुणो भागो रूपै कदे शसं युक्तो वा
      अर्िधतः स वगर्ः स्याद् यो राशे िस्त्रगुणो भागः ॥१२०॥
     रूपै कदे शसं युक्तोऽथवा स घनः स्यात् तस्य मूलं
     यत् तद् घनमूलं प्रोक्तं तत् स्यात् सममूलकप्रमाणम् ॥१२१॥
     वा. भा.---यो राशे िस्त्रगुणो भागो रूपै कदे शसं युक्तो वा अर्िधतः स वगर्ः
     स्यात् । यो राशे िस्त्रगुणो भागो रूपै कदे शसं युक्तोऽथवा स घनः स्यात्
     तस्य मूलं यत् तद् घनमूलं प्रोक्तं तत् सममूलकप्रमाणम् स्यात् ॥१२०-
     -१२१॥

                                               चन्द्रसूयग
                                                        र् िणतम् --- ितिथयोगनक्षत्रानयनम्

चन्द्राच्च स्पष्टात् सूयर्ं शोध्यं यद् भवित शे षं तत्
द्वादशभागै हृत्र् वा ितथयः स्युः स्फुटास्तदधार्त् ॥१०९॥
ितथयो द्वादशाङ्गुल्यः सूयर्चन्द्रयोरन्तरात् ताः
ितथीनां पञ्चदशिभः सं युक्तं योगिनर्िमितः ॥११०॥
योगः सूयर्चन्द्रयो रािशसं युक्त्यं शकलाित्मका यः
स तै ः पञ्चदशिभः सं युक्तः सप्तिंवशत्या हृतः ॥१११॥
     वा. भा.---चन्द्रात् स्पष्टात् सूयर्ं शोध्य यद् भवित शे षं तत्
     द्वादशभागै ः हृत्वा ितथयः स्युः स्फुटास्तदधार्त्। ितथयो द्वादशाङ्गुल्यः
     सूयर्चन्द्रयोरन्तरात् ितथीनां पञ्चदशिभः सं युक्तं योगिनर्िमितः सूयर्चन्द्रयो
     रािशसं युक्त्यं शकलाित्मका सा सप्तिंवशत्या हृता नक्षत्रं स्यात् ॥१०९-
     -१११॥
     चन्द्राच्च स्पष्टात् सूयर्ं शोध्यं यद् भवित शे षं तत्
     िंत्रशता हृत्वा नक्षत्रं तत् स्यात् स्फुटं रािशमानम् ॥११२॥
     सूयर्स्य गितः स्पष्टा चन्द्रस्यािप गितः स्पष्टा तयोः
     िवशे षः स्यात् ितथ्यधर्भुिक्तिरयं प्रकीर्ितता ॥११३॥
     वा. भा.---चन्द्रात् स्पष्टात् सूयर्ं शोध्य यद् भवित शे षं तत् िंत्रशता हृत्वा
     नक्षत्रं तत् स्यात् स्फुटं रािशमानम् । सूयर्स्य गितः स्पष्टा चन्द्रस्यािप
     गितः स्पष्टा तयोर्िवशे षः स्यात् ितथ्यधर्भुिक्तः प्रकीर्ितता ॥११२--११३॥
                                                       गोलानयनम् --- भारतीययवनक्रमः

यवनानां मते िबम्बं भूगोलं िनरक्षदे शे िस्थतम्
िनजदे शोच्चं तन्मध्यं तत्रै व िक्षितजं वृत्तम् ॥११४॥
भारतीयमते त्वक्षवृत्तं मध्यमण्डलं तत्
िक्षितजं तन्महद् वृत्तं समरे खािप तत्रै व ॥११५॥
उन्नतजीवाकोिटज्यािह्रया हृज्या भुजो नतज्या वा
कणार्क्षेत्रावृत्ते व्यासाधर्ं द्व्यमतोद्यन्न ॥११६॥
    वा. भा.---यवनानां मते िबम्बं भूगोलं िनरक्षदे शे िस्थतं िनजदे शोच्चं
    तन्मध्यं तत्रै व िक्षितजं वृत्तम् । भारतीयमते तु अक्षवृत्तं मध्यमण्डलं
    तत् िक्षितजं तन्महद् वृत्तं समरे खािप तत्रै व। उभयोरिप मतयोः सामान्यं
    गोलज्ञानमुभयमतोद्यन्निमत्युच्यते ॥११४--११६॥


                                                                 चन्द्रग्रहणािधकारः


     अस्याध्यायस्य िवषयः --- चन्द्रग्रहणस्य कालः, मानं , तथा आकलनं ।
     स्पशर्प्रथमस्पशर्मध्यमोक्षाणां िनधार्रणं ।

                                                                     श्लोक ७०--७१

िद्वगितर्िदनिनमार्थं स्फुटचन्द्रोदये तु यत् िपण्डम् ।
तत् स्यात् स्पशर्िपण्डं ततो ग्रहणमध्यसाधनम् ॥७०॥
तत् स्यात् स्पशर्िपण्डं ततो ग्रहणमध्यसाधनम् ।
तद्िद्वतीये न वा कालो ग्रहणस्य प्रकीर्िततः ॥७१॥
      िव. भा.---िद्व गितः िदनिनमार्थं स्फुटचन्द्रोदये तु यत् िपण्डं तत् स्यात्
      स्पशर्िपण्डं ततो ग्रहणमध्यसाधनम् । तद्िद्वतीये न वा कालो ग्रहणस्य
      प्रकीर्िततः ॥७०--७१॥

                                                                      श्लोक ७२--७३

चन्द्रसूयर्योरन्तरं याम्यायनं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा।
दिक्षणोत्तरयोः क्रान्त्योरन्तरं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा ॥७२॥
याम्योत्तरयोः क्रान्त्योरन्तरं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा।
तद्भुजज्या तु करणं तत् स्पशर्मोक्षौ प्रदशर्येत् ॥७३॥
     िव. भा.---चन्द्रसूयर्योरन्तरं याम्यायनं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा। दिक्षणोत्तरयः
     क्रान्त्योरन्तरं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा। याम्योत्तरयोः क्रान्त्योरन्तरं तत् क्षयं
     वृिंद्ध वा। तद्भुजज्या तु करणं तत् स्पशर्मोक्षौ प्रदशर्येत् ॥७२--७३॥
                                                            श्लोक ७४--८०
याम्योत्तरयोः क्रान्त्योरन्तरं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा।
तद्भुजज्या तु करणं तत् स्पशर्मोक्षौ प्रदशर्येत् ॥७४॥
तद्भुजज्या तु करणं तत् स्पशर्मोक्षौ प्रदशर्येत्।
तद्िद्वतीये न वा कालो ग्रहणस्य प्रकीर्िततः ॥७५॥
स्पशर्मोक्षौ यदा स्यातां तदा ग्रहणं प्रकीर्िततम् ।
तद्िद्वतीये न वा कालो ग्रहणस्य प्रकीर्िततः ॥७६॥
तद्िद्वतीये न वा कालो ग्रहणस्य प्रकीर्िततः।
तद्भुजज्या तु करणं तत् स्पशर्मोक्षौ प्रदशर्येत् ॥७७॥


                                                             ग्रहणसूत्रािण
स्पशर्िपण्डं ततो ग्रहणमध्यसाधनम् ।
तद्िद्वतीये न वा कालो ग्रहणस्य प्रकीर्िततः ॥७८॥
चन्द्रसूयर्योरन्तरं याम्यायनं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा।
दिक्षणोत्तरयोः क्रान्त्योरन्तरं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा ॥७९॥
याम्योत्तरयोः क्रान्त्योरन्तरं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा।
तद्भुजज्या तु करणं तत् स्पशर्मोक्षौ प्रदशर्येत् ॥८०॥


                                                             ग्रहणानयनम्
िद्वगितर्िदनिनमार्थं स्फुटचन्द्रोदये तु यत् िपण्डम् ।
तत् स्यात् स्पशर्िपण्डं ततो ग्रहणमध्यसाधनम् ॥८१॥
तत् स्यात् स्पशर्िपण्डं ततो ग्रहणमध्यसाधनम् ।
तद्िद्वतीये न वा कालो ग्रहणस्य प्रकीर्िततः ॥८२॥
चन्द्रसूयर्योरन्तरं याम्यायनं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा।
दिक्षणोत्तरयोः क्रान्त्योरन्तरं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा ॥८३॥
याम्योत्तरयोः क्रान्त्योरन्तरं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा।
तद्भुजज्या तु करणं तत् स्पशर्मोक्षौ प्रदशर्येत् ॥८४॥


                                                             ग्रहणमध्यम्
स्पशर्मोक्षौ यदा स्यातां तदा ग्रहणं प्रकीर्िततम् ।
तद्िद्वतीये न वा कालो ग्रहणस्य प्रकीर्िततः ॥८५॥
तद्िद्वतीये न वा कालो ग्रहणस्य प्रकीर्िततः।
तद्भुजज्या तु करणं तत् स्पशर्मोक्षौ प्रदशर्येत् ॥८६॥
स्पशर्िपण्डं ततो ग्रहणमध्यसाधनम् ।
तद्िद्वतीये न वा कालो ग्रहणस्य प्रकीर्िततः ॥८७॥
                                                          सूयग्र
                                                             र् हणम् --- रिवग्रहणानयनम्

सूयर्ग्रहणे तु लम्बनं शोध्यं चन्द्रसूयर्योरन्तरात्
तत् शुद्धं स्यात् स्पशार्रम्भे मोक्षे तथै व ॥१२२॥
लम्बनात् स्पशर्कालः स्पशर्े युक्तो मोक्षे शुद्धः
ग्रहणमध्यं तु तयोयार्े गाधर्े नैव िसध्यित ॥१२३॥
चन्द्रस्य पिरवे षो यः सूयर्स्य तु िवशे षणात्
तत् स्याद् ग्रहणमानं तत् प्रकीर्िततिमहाचायर्ै ः ॥१२४॥
       वा. भा.---सूयर्ग्रहणे तु लम्बनं शोध्यं चन्द्रसूयर्योरन्तरात् तत् शुद्धं स्यात्
       स्पशार्रम्भे मोक्षे तथै व। लम्बनात् स्पशर्कालः स्पशर्े युक्तो मोक्षे शुद्धः
       ग्रहणमध्यं तु तयोयार्े गाधर्े नैव िसध्यित। चन्द्रस्य पिरवे षो यः सूयर्स्य
       तु िवशे षणात् तत् स्याद् ग्रहणमानं तत् प्रकीर्िततिमहाचायर्ै ः ॥१२२--
       १२४॥

                                                        लम्बनम् --- दे शान्तरलम्बनानयनम्

स्वदे शलम्बज्ञाया द्युज्याया चान्तरं यत् स्यात्
तत् ित्रज्यया हृत्वा लब्धं लम्बनं तत् प्रकीर्िततम् ॥१२५॥
लम्बज्या द्युज्यया हृत्वा ित्रज्यां गुणये त् ततः
लब्धं तत् स्यात् लम्बनं तत् प्राचीनै रुक्तमीश्वरै ः ॥१२६॥
तज्जं लम्बनजातं यत् तत् कालहृतं स्फुटं स्यात्
तद्धतृ ं नाडीषु रूपं स्यादे तल्लम्बनसाधनम् ॥१२७॥
       वा. भा.---स्वदे शलम्बज्ञाया द्युज्याया चान्तरं यत् स्यात् तत् ित्रज्यया
       हृत्वा लब्धं लम्बनं तत् प्रकीर्िततम् । लम्बज्या द्युज्यया हृत्वा ित्रज्यां
       गुणये त् ततो लब्धं तत् स्यात् लम्बनं तत् प्राचीनै रुक्तम् तज्जं
       लम्बनजातं यत् तत् कालहृतं स्फुटं स्यात् तद्धतृ ं नाडीषु रूपं
       स्यादे तल्लम्बनसाधनम् ॥१२५--१२७॥


                                                                   ग्रहणस्पशर्-मोक्षयोः
चन्द्रसूयर्योरन्तरं याम्यायनं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा।
दिक्षणोत्तरयोः क्रान्त्योरन्तरं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा ॥८८॥
याम्योत्तरयोः क्रान्त्योरन्तरं तत् क्षयं वृिंद्ध वा।
तद्भुजज्या तु करणं तत् स्पशर्मोक्षौ प्रदशर्येत् ॥८९॥
तद्भुजज्या तु करणं तत् स्पशर्मोक्षौ प्रदशर्येत्।
तद्िद्वतीये न वा कालो ग्रहणस्य प्रकीर्िततः ॥९०॥
सङ्क्षे पः
एतिस्मन् खण्डे िनम्निलिखताः श्लोकाः समावे िशताः:
 स्पष्टािधकारः (श्लोक १२--५१)
 ित्रप्रश्नािधकारः (श्लोक ५४--६९)
 छायाकरणी (श्लोक २८--३६)
 लम्बाक्षज्यानयनम् (श्लोक ५७--६२)
 प्राणगिणतम् (श्लोक १२८--१३०)

 लग्ननयनम् (श्लोक १३४--१३६)

 ज्योत्पित्तः (श्लोक १००--१०३)

 पाटीगिणतम् (श्लोक ११७--१२१)
 ितिथयोगनक्षत्रम् (श्लोक १०९--११३)

    समािप्तिववरणम्
    एषः िद्वतीयभागस्य िद्वतीयखण्डः समाप्तः। अत्र समावे िशतं सािहत्यं
    िनम्निलिखताध्यायान् समे ित:
          1. स्पष्टािधकारः --- ग्रहाणां मध्यमात् स्पष्टस्थानस्य गणनम्
          2. ित्रप्रश्नािधकारः --- लम्बननाडीछायागणनम्
          3. चन्द्रग्रहणािधकारः --- चन्द्रग्रहणकालमानाकलनम्
          4. गिणतीयसूत्रािण --- ज्योत्पित्तः, गौरीगुप्ता, पाटीगिणतम्
    ब्रह्मगुप्तस्याः िसद्धान्ते प्रत्ये कं िनयमः सं स्कृतपद्ये षु (आयार्यां )
    उपस्थािपतः, ततो िवस्तृतव्याख्या (व्याख्या) अनुसरित। गिणतीयसूत्रािण
    भारतीयज्यापद्धिंत प्रयुञ्जते तथा त्रै रािशकं ज्यािमतीयं च तकर्ं
    प्रयुञ्जते ।

Editorial Preface
This bilingual edition presents the Sanskrit text of Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta Part II, Chunk 3, with facing English
translations. The layout follows the standard format for critical editions of Sanskrit scientific texts:

    • Left column: Sanskrit text in Devanagari script, as found in the source edition.

    • Right column: English translation with IAST romanization of technical terms.

    The commentary (टीका) by Sudhākara Dvivedī follows the traditional Indian pañcāṅga structure of:

   1. Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — The original verse in āryā or anuṣṭubh metre.

   2. Sūtrārtha (सूत्राथर्ः) — Literal meaning of the verse.

   3. Hindi Bhāṣya (िहन्दी भाष्य) — Explanation in Hindi.

   4. Upapatti (उपपित्तः) — Mathematical proof or demonstration.
Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta — Bilingual Edition — Part II, Chunk 3                                                         3



1 चन्द्रग्रहणिवकारः — Candragrahaṇavikāraḥ (Lunar Eclipse Variations)


Introduction
This chapter (चन्द्रग्रहणिवकारः) deals with the computation of lunar eclipses, following Brahmagupta’s exposition
in the ब्राह्मस्फुटिसद्धान्त. The commentary (सुधाकर-टीका) by Paṇḍita Kṛpāludatta Sūnu Sudhākara Dvivedī provides
elaborate explanations in Hindi, with mathematical demonstrations (उपपित्त).



1.1 Verses 11–12: Computation of इष्टग्रास (Desired Eclipse Magnitude)

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verses 11–12:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
     इ
                                                                                                          –
    Translation: “The half-duration (sthitidala) diminished by the desired quantity, multiplied by the daily
motion (bhukti) and divided by the deflection (villepa), gives the bhuja (sine). The koṭi (cosine) is computed
by the rule of the desired quantity (iṣṭeṇu). From the half-sum of the diameters (mānaikyārdha), the karṇa
(hypotenuse) being subtracted, the result gives the true obscuration (channa-māna).”
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Sūtrārtha (सूत्राथर्ः):
     इ

    Literal Meaning: The verse gives the method for computing the iṣṭagrāsa (desired eclipse magnitude).
From the sthitidala (half-duration) diminished by the desired quantity, multiplied by the bhukti (daily motion)
and divided by the villepa (deflection), one gets the bhuja (sine). The koṭi (cosine) is computed by the rule of iṣṭeṇu
(desired quantity). From the mānaikyārdha (half-sum of diameters), the karṇa (hypotenuse) being subtracted,
the result gives the true obscuration.
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Hindi Bhāṣya (िहन्दी भाष्य):
     इ                                                                                      ,                   ,
                                                                                                  (             )
                                     ,                (                                           )
                                                                    –
     English Translation of Hindi Commentary: “Subtracting the desired (iṣṭa) from the sthitidala, multiply-
ing by the bhukti and dividing by the villepa, one obtains the bhuja. By applying this result to the motions of
the Sun, Moon, and node (pāta), one computes the instantaneous lunar latitude (tātkālika candra-śara), which
is the koṭi. The square root of the sum of their squares is the karṇa. From the mānaikyārdha (sum of the radii of
the eclipsed and eclipsing bodies), subtracting the karṇa, the remainder is the desired magnitude (iṣṭagrāsa).”
  c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Upapatti (उपपित्तः) — Proof:
     स                                                -
                  ,
                                                                                ,                               ,
                                                                                     (                )

Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta — Bilingual Edition — Part II, Chunk 3                                                    4


    English Translation of Proof: “After the contact (sparśī), for whatever desired number of ghaṭīs the
knowledge of the grāsa is required, that desired number of ghaṭīs should be subtracted from the sthityardha.
The remainder is used in proportion. If in sixty ghaṭīs the difference in motion between the Sun and Moon gives
a certain number of minutes (kalā), then [by proportion] in the iṣṭa-diminished sthityardha ghaṭīs, whatever
result is obtained is the bhuja. From the result obtained by proportion, by applying the motions of the Sun,
Moon, and node, the instantaneous lunar latitude (tātkālika candra-vāra) is computed — that is the koṭi. The
square root of the sum of the squares of these two (bhuja and koṭi) is the karṇa. Subtracting the karṇa from the
mānaikyārdha gives the desired grāsa.”

2 सूयर्ग्रहणािधकारः — Sūryagrahaṇādhikāraḥ (Solar Eclipse Chapter)


    This chapter (सूयर्ग्रहणािधकारः, the fifth chapter of the text) deals with the computation of solar eclipses. The
chapter begins with the statement of purpose (प्रयोजन) and proceeds through the computation of the five ज्या
(sines) required for eclipse calculation: उदयज्या, मध्यज्या, रिवदयाकणर्, िविशमशङ्कु, and हृक्षे प.

2.1 Verse 1: Statement of Purpose

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verse 1:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg

      अ                                                                                —
      ग


     Introduction: “Now the chapter on solar eclipse is begun. First, the purpose of its commencement is stated
—”
    Translation: “Because the inter-planetary [distance] computed by the grahabhāskara [method] previously
is an obstruction in the inter-planetary [computation], therefore I shall state the computation of dyuti (parallax)
with its own degrees, so that there may be agreement.”
    Technical note: The grahabhāskara refers to the method given by earlier astronomers (like Bhāskara I) for
computing the distance between planets, which Brahmagupta finds to be defective for solar eclipse computation.
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Sūtrārtha (सूत्राथर्ः):
      प

                                                                   -




    Literal Meaning: “Previously, in the eastern horizon (pūrve kṣitije), by the degrees of the grahabhāskara
method, the planets become visible or invisible (haśya-āhaśya) in their own time-degrees. Later, in the western
horizon (paścima-kṣitije), by the degrees of the planetary distances, [the same] according to number. Therefore, I
shall state that computation — the computation of the praharāntara. That is: in the eastern horizon, on whatever
day after the rising of the planet, the rising of the Sun [occurs] after a certain number of time-ghaṭīs, on that
day the planet becomes invisible (haśya) in the remaining night. Similarly, in the western horizon, after the
setting of the Sun, when the setting [occurs] after a certain number of time-ghaṭīs, on that day in the evening
the planet’s invisibility (haśyatva) [is determined].”
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Hindi Bhāṣya (िहन्दी भाष्य):
      ज                                           (        ,                   ,               ,           ,       )
                                                                                   (
              )                                                (                                               )
                                                                                           ,           –   ,
      –                     ,               –         ,                –                           ,
                                                                           ,
Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta — Bilingual Edition — Part II, Chunk 3                                                     7


    English Translation: “For whatever reason, in solar eclipses, the five jyās (udayajyā, madhyajyā, ravi-
dayākarṇa, viśimaśaṅku, hṛkṣepa) — by which earlier teachers like Bhāskara computed the prāṇayana — from
that, the gaṇitaikya (agreement between observed and computed values) is not obtained. For that reason, so
that their agreement may exist, I state the prāṇayana [method]. The tithi-anta [refers to] the relation with the
graha: udayajyā [refers to] parallax (bhramā), madhyajyā [to] the sine of the zenith-distance of the tenth degree
(daśama-lagna-natāṃśajyā), viśimaśaṅku [to] the ascensional difference (hagati), hṛkṣepa [to] the sine of the
zenith-distance of the nonagesimal (viśima-natāṃśajyā). In the Sūryasiddhānta too, the solar eclipse prāṇayana
is by the aforesaid five jyās. In the Pauliśasiddhānta, in solar eclipses, separate five jyās of the Moon are com-
puted; therefore there the solar eclipse is accomplished by the two-jyā method.”

2.2 Verses 2–3: Conditions for लम्बन (Deflection) and अवनित (Depression)

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verses 2–3:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
     व

     अ


    Translation — Verse 2: “When one [planet] is equal to the viśrima-lagna (nonagesimal ascendant), there is
no lambana (deflection in longitude). When it is greater or less than that, lambana occurs. When its declination-
sine (krāntijyā) is equal to the latitude-sine (akṣajyā), then there is no avanati (depression).”
    Translation — Verse 3: “Otherwise, avanati occurs. When both lambana and avanati are possible, then
the rising [point] equal to the lagna, the ghaṭikās elapsed since its rising, its śaṅku (gnomon), and the prāṇas of
its motion — [are computed] thereafter.”
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Sūtrārtha (सूत्राथर्ः):
     त
                                                                             !


                                                  -

                 –
    Literal Meaning: “At the end of the tithi, at the time of computed conjunction (gaṇitāgata-darśāntikāle),
having computed the lagna by the previous method stated in the Tripraśna, the viśrima [nonagesimal] is to be
computed. When the Sun is equal to one [point] with that viśrimalagna, there is no lambana. When the Sun is
greater or less than that viśrima, lambana occurs — this meaning is self-evident. Similarly, when the northern
declination-sine (krāntijyā) of that viśrima is equal to the latitude-sine (akṣajyā) of one’s own place, then there
is no avanati; otherwise avanati occurs. When both lambana and avanati are possible, then having made the
rising equal to the lagna — the rāśyudaya-eṣi-lagna of one’s own place — by the method stated in the Tripraśna:
‘the first diminished by the bhoga, increased by the bhukti, [gives] the middle udaya’, etc., the ghaṭikās elapsed
since the rising of that viśrima are to be computed.”

3 चन्द्रच्छायािधकारः — Candracchāyādhikāraḥ (Moon’s Shadow Chapter)


    This chapter (चन्द्रच्छायािधकारः) deals with the computation of the Moon’s shadow, which is essential for un-
derstanding lunar phenomena. The shadow of the Earth falls on the Moon during a lunar eclipse, and its com-
putation involves calculating the च्छाया (shadow), मध्यच्छाया (middle shadow), and related quantities.

3.1 Verse 1: Beginning of the Chapter

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verse 1:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg

     अ                                                                         —
     प


     Introduction: “Now the chapter on the Moon’s shadow is begun. First, the purpose of its commencement
is stated —”
     Translation: “Previously, the difference between the Moon and the lagna, and afterwards, that of the sapta-
lagna etc., because from that the shadow of the Moon (indu-ccchāyā) is daily [computed], therefore I shall state
the computation (anayana) of that.”
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Sūtrārtha (सूत्राथर्ः):
     प        (                 )                        ,             (               )
                                             (                 )                   (           )           ,
                                                                   (       )               (        )
                            “                                                              ”

    Literal Meaning: “Previously (in the eastern horizon), the difference between the Moon and the lagna, and
afterwards (in the western horizon), the difference between the saptalagna and the Moon, and daily by effort
— by which cause the Moon’s shadow (indu-ccchāyā) is known, and from the Moon’s shadow that difference is
[also] known — therefore I shall state that computation (anayana), the shadow-computation (chāyā-anayana).
In the Siddhāntaśekhara: ‘Usually the difference between the diminished-Sun-lagna and the lagna-nāśin in the
west’ etc. — by this, what was spoken by Śrīpati-nācārya is here spoken in the same manner.”
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Hindi Bhāṣya (िहन्दी भाष्य):
     अ                                                   ,                     -
                                                  ,
                                                                                       (
                        )

    English Translation: “Now the chapter on the Moon’s shadow is begun. First, its purpose is stated. The
difference between the Moon and the lagna in the eastern horizon, and the difference between the saptalagna and
the Moon in the western horizon — because from these, daily, the Moon’s shadow is known (first, the knowledge
of the Moon’s shadow is obtained), and from the Moon’s shadow the knowledge of that difference is obtained
— therefore I state the shadow-computation.”
Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta — Bilingual Edition — Part II, Chunk 3                                              10


3.2 Verse 2: Shadow Computation

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verse 2:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg

     प


    Translation: “The parts of the upright-time-difference (ūrdhvakālika-udbala-naṣṭa-sama-sthiti-pāta) [hav-
ing] the form of a prabhahṛṇḍa [spherical triangle], the semi-diameter (vyāsa) of the shadow at the horizon
(tala-lagna), the proximity to its own agra — there is no abbreviation (saṅkṣepaḥ).”
    Note: This is a technical verse whose exact interpretation requires the accompanying commentary. The
prabhahṛṇḍa is a type of spherical triangle used in Indian astronomy.

3.3 Verses 3–4: Detailed Shadow Calculation

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verses 3–4:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg

     इ                       —

     प


     म


    Introduction to Verse 3: “Now [the author] states what is to be done —”
    Translation — Verse 3: [Same structure as verse 2, establishing the method.]
    Translation — Verse 4: “As the root (mūla) diminished by the root, or the elapsed [time], having been
extracted, another [quantity] comes therefrom. Divided by the own motion (svabhakti), that is the desired
shadow-motion (abhīṣṭa-ccchāyā-gati). That same [quantity], extracted from the root (mūlodḍhṛtā).”
    Note: This verse describes an iterative root-extraction method for computing the rate of change of the
shadow. The mūla here refers to a base quantity from which differences are taken.

4 ग्रहोदयास्तािधकारः — Grahodayāstādhikāraḥ (Planetary Rising and Setting)


    This chapter (ग्रहोदयास्तािधकारः) deals with the computation of the rising and setting times of planets. The
chapter explains how to determine the times when planets become visible (उदय, heliacal rising) and invisible
(अस्त, heliacal setting) based on their elongation from the Sun.

4.1 Verse 1: Pratyaya (Introduction)

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verse 1:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg

     अ                                                                     —
     ग


    Introduction: “Now the chapter on planetary rising and setting is begun. First, the purpose of its com-
mencement is stated —”
    Translation: “Because previously, in the inter-planetary computation, the grahabhāskara distance is an
obstruction (bādhaka), therefore, with its own degrees, so that there may be agreement — I state the computation
of dyuti.”
    Note: This verse serves as a parallel to the solar eclipse chapter’s opening, establishing that Brahmagupta’s
method supersedes earlier approaches that failed to achieve agreement (aiṃya) between computation and ob-
servation.
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Sūtrārtha (सूत्राथर्ः):
     प

                                                               -




    Literal Meaning: “Previously, in the eastern horizon, by the grahabhāskara distances in their own degrees,
in their own time-degrees, later in the western horizon, by the planetary distances in their own time-degrees,
according to number, the planets become visible or invisible (haśya-āhaśya). Therefore, I shall state that com-
putation — the computation of the praharāntara. That is: in the eastern horizon, on whatever day after the
rising of the planet, the rising of the Sun occurs after a certain number of time-ghaṭīs, on that day the planet
becomes invisible (haśya) in the remaining night. Similarly, in the western horizon, after the setting of the Sun,
when the setting occurs after a certain number of time-ghaṭīs, on that day in the evening the planet’s invisibility
(haśyatva) [is established].”

4.2 Verses 8–9: Daily Rising and Setting

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verses 8–9:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg

     त
                                                     (                 )
                             (           )
                                         ,                (        )
Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta — Bilingual Edition — Part II, Chunk 3                                                    12


    Translation — Verse 8: “By iterative computation (asakṛt-karma) from the instantaneous (tātkālika) planet-
lagna, the daily rising and setting are to be computed. First, at whatever time the grahodita-ghaṭī [elapsed ghaṭīs
since planetary rising] is to be known, the lagna at that time (tātkālika lagna) and the planetary rising-lagna
are to be computed. Computing the difference-ghaṭīs of both, by those (computed ghaṭīs) moving the planet,
again computing its rising-lagna, from the instantaneous fixed lagna again computing the desired ghaṭīs — in
this manner, iteratively (asakṛt, repeatedly) doing [this].”
    Note: This verse describes the iterative procedure (asakṛt-karma) for refining the computation of a planet’s
rising time. The method involves successive approximation until the computed value stabilizes.

4.3 Verse 9: Moon’s Rising and Setting Computation

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verse 9:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg

     उ


    Translation: “The rising and setting of the Moon (indu) are to be computed by time-degrees (kālāṃśa) equal
to digits (aṅka-saṃmitaiḥ). If there is diminution (hīnatva) or excess (adhikatva), in that interval the yoga-kāla
[conjunction time] would be [obtained].”
    Note: The aṅka (digit) is a unit equal to 12 degrees. The verse states that the Moon’s rising and setting are
computed using time-degrees measured in these units.
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Sūtrārtha (सूत्राथर्ः):
     अ



    Literal Meaning: “By time-degrees equal to digits (aṅka), that is, by twelve time-degrees (dvādaśa-kālāṃśa),
the rising and setting of the Moon (indu = candra) are to be computed. Here, if the computed time-degree
(paṭhita-kālāṃśa) has diminution or excess from the desired time-degree (iṣṭa-kālāṃśa), then in that interval,
between those two computed time-degrees, the yoga-kāla (conjunction time), or yogavat-kāla (time like con-
junction), would be [obtained], as clearly stated in the seventh verse.”
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Hindi Bhāṣya (िहन्दी भाष्य):
     अ
                                       (          )

    English Translation: “By time-degrees equal to digits (aṅka), that is, by time-degrees equal to twelve, the
rising and setting are to be computed. If the computed time-degree has diminution (hīnatva, being smaller)
or excess (adhikatva) from the desired time-degree, then in that interval, between those two time-degrees, the
yoga-kāla (conjunction time) would be [obtained].”
Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta — Bilingual Edition — Part II, Chunk 3                                                  13



5 शुक्लोन्नत्यिधकारः — Śuklonnatyadhikāraḥ (The Brightness of the Moon)


    This chapter (शुक्लोन्नत्यिधकारः) deals with the computation of the illuminated portion of the Moon’s disk —
the शुक्ल (bright) portion. The chapter explains how to determine the amount of the Moon’s surface that is
illuminated by the Sun at any given time.

5.1 Verses 1–3: Sphuṭaśuklonnati Computation

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verse 4:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
     अ
     र

    Introduction: “Now the chapter on the Moon’s bright elevation (śuklonnati) is begun.”
    Translation — Verse 4: “The ghaṭikās [which are] the khacarārdha (half of the celestial sphere’s motion) —
by the Sun, Moon, node, and lagna, and from the rising of the own declination (sva-krānty-udaya), [which are]
the remaining [ghaṭikās] elapsed from the own lagna — having made [the computation] from the setting of the
Sun, [these are] to be computed.”
    Note: The khacarārdha refers to half the diurnal motion of the celestial sphere. The verse establishes the
parameters needed to compute the Moon’s illumination.
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Sūtrārtha (सूत्राथर्ः):
    ख




    Literal Meaning: “The khacarārdha ghaṭikās [which are] from the setting of the Sun (ravyastāt), [which
are] the remaining [ghaṭikās] elapsed from the own-lagna of the Moon — or the elapsed/deficient ghaṭikās of the
Moon’s rising-lagna — are to be computed. By what? By the Sun, Moon, node, lagna, and from the rising of the
own declination. Here, this is stated: at the time of the Sun’s setting, the Sun, Moon, node, and lagna. Having
computed these, by the own declination (sva-krānti), by the Moon’s declination (candra-krānti), from the rising,
[computing] the elapsed or deficient ghaṭikās of the Moon’s rising-lagna from the udaya-lagna by the method
‘diminished by the bhoga, increased by the bhukti, [gives] the middle udaya’, etc. After the Sun’s setting, the
ghaṭikās by which the Moon sets — those are the elapsed ghaṭikās. Before the Sun’s setting, the ghaṭikās by
which the Moon sets — those are the deficient ghaṭikās. Similarly, in the eastern horizon, from the Sun’s rising,
the elapsed or deficient ghaṭikās of the Moon’s rising are to be computed — this is to be understood from the
meaning.”
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Hindi Bhāṣya (िहन्दी भाष्य):
    त
                                                        (              )
                            (          )
                                   ,                (          )   ,
     (                )
Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta — Bilingual Edition — Part II, Chunk 3                                                    14


    English Translation: “By iterative computation (asakṛt-karma) from the instantaneous planet-lagna, the
daily rising and setting are to be computed. First, at whatever time the planetary elapsed ghaṭīs are to be
known, the lagna at that time (tātkālika lagna) and the planetary rising-lagna are to be computed. Computing
the difference-ghaṭīs of both, by those (computed ghaṭīs) moving the planet, again computing its rising-lagna,
from the instantaneous fixed lagna again computing the desired ghaṭīs — in this manner, iteratively (asakṛt,
repeatedly) doing [this]. And for the knowledge of the sthita-kāla, computation from the sthita-lagna (sapta-
bhastala-lagna) should be done. In this way, from the first day of lunar visibility (candra darśana) up to the full
moon (pūrṇimā), on the desired day, the setting time from the Sun’s setting and the rising time from the Sun’s
rising are to be computed.”

5.2 Verse 5: The शुक्लोन्नित (Bright Elevation)

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verse 5:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg

     र


    Translation — Verse 5: [Recapitulates the parameters for computing the bright elevation.]
    Technical summary: The verse reiterates that by the Sun, Moon, node, lagna, and from the rising of the dec-
lination, the khacarārdha ghaṭikās from the Sun’s setting are to be used to compute the Moon’s bright elevation.
  c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Upapatti (उपपित्तः):
     य




                                                         “
                                                                                                        ”
    English Translation of Proof: “On whatever day, after the Sun’s setting, the Moon sets after an additional
number of time-ghaṭīs. Before the Sun’s rising, the Moon rises [after a certain number of ghaṭīs]. On that very
day, when the Moon is visible, the two bright elevations (śva-jy-unnati) are to be computed. The bright elevation
of the bright [half] (sita-śuklonnati) is half the disk (bimbārdha) of the Moon’s bright [portion]. Thus Bhāskara
computed the śuklonnati in the first pāda of the month-end. In the second and third pādas, half the disk is dark
(kṛṣṇa). Therefore there the dark śuklonnati occurs. Thus by the teacher (ācārya), two śuklonnatis are computed.
But here, the pāda discussion is not made. Bhāskara, having considered in his mind that the dark śuklonnati is
not clearly observable, computed only the bright śuklonnati. And it is stated: ‘Brahmagupta and others have
produced the dark śuklonnati also for the second and third caraṇas; that is not approved by me. For neither is
the dark śuklonnati clearly perceived by men.’

5.3 Verses 11–12: स्पष्टशुक्ल (True Bright Portion) Computation

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verses 11–12:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg

     त


   Translation — Verse 11: “Or when the middle motion (madhyagati) is multiplied by its own shadow (tac-
chāyā) and by the semi-diameter (sva-kara) multiplied by the tithi — the results are additive or subtractive. In
Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta — Bilingual Edition — Part II, Chunk 3                                                       15


equality (sāmya), the difference is [taken]; in direction-difference (dig-anyatva), [it is] the avanati [depression]
expressed in digits (aṅkya).”
   Note: This verse gives the method for computing the true bright portion (spaṣṭa-śukla) of the Moon. The
madhyagati is the mean daily motion, and the tacchāyā is the shadow corresponding to the planet’s altitude.
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Sūtrārtha (सूत्राथर्ः):
     ह
                                                -        -     -

    Literal Meaning: “In the hṛjya [radius = 3438’] of the Sun and Moon, the kṣepī [sine of latitude], having
been multiplied by the respective middle motion and by the semi-diameter (vyāsadala) multiplied by the tithi
and by fifteen [i.e., by the tithi-multiplied semi-diameter multiplied by 15], divided by the trijyā [radius = 3438’],
separately, separately, the natī [depressions] of the Sun and Moon are obtained. Or, their middle motion, their
kṣepa-equal hṛjya, the shadow being multiplied by that, multiplied by the tithi-multiplied own shadow-karman,
their difference is [taken].”
  c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Upapatti (उपपित्तः):
     प                “                                                            ”

   English Translation of Proof: “The first computation [is made] accurate by the method of Bhāskara:
‘Having multiplied by the sahakṣepa [combined latitude] of the Moon, by the own middle motion-tithi-aṃśa-
multiplied [quantity]’ etc.”
Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta — Bilingual Edition — Part II, Chunk 3                                                                   16



6 चन्द्रशुक्लोन्नत्यिधकारः — Chandraśuklonnatyadhikāraḥ (Moon’s Bright Eleva-
  tion)


     This is the seventh अध्याय (chapter) of the ब्राह्मस्फुटिसद्धान्त, dealing with the Moon’s bright elevation (चन्द्रशुक्लोन्नित).
It comprises eighteen verses (श्लोक) and covers the computation of the arc of illumination on the Moon’s disk.

6.1 Verses 1–2: Introduction

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verses 1–2:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg

      अ
      प

      म

    Introduction: “Now the beginning of the chapter on the Moon’s bright elevation (candra-śukla-unnati).”
    Translation — Verse 1: “The parts of the upright-time-difference (ūrdhvakālika-udbala-naṣṭa-sama-sthiti-
pāta) [having] the form of a prāgrahuṇḍa [spherical triangle], the semi-diameter (vyāsa) of the shadow at the
horizon (tala-lagna), the proximity to its own agra — there is no abbreviation (saṅkṣepaḥ).”
    Translation — Verse 2: “As the root (mūla) diminished by the root, or the elapsed [time], having been
extracted, another [quantity] comes therefrom. Divided by the own motion (svabhakti), that is the desired
shadow-motion (abhīṣṭa-ccchāyā-gati). That same [quantity], extracted from the root (mūlodḍhṛtā).”
    Note: Verse 2 establishes an iterative root-extraction algorithm for computing the rate of change of the
shadow, which is essential for determining the Moon’s illumination over time.

6.2 Verse 18: Chapter Conclusion

Sūtra (सूत्रम् ) — Verse 18:
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg

      इ                             —
      इ

    Introduction: “Now [the author] states the conclusion of the chapter —”
    Translation — Verse 18: “Thus, in [the topics of] bāhu (base), koṭi (perpendicular), karṇa (hypotenuse),
spaṣṭa (true [longitude]), sita (bright [part]), parilekha (outline), sūtra (line), and kardama [mud] — by eighteen
doors (dvāra), [this is] the seventh chapter [on] the Moon’s bright elevation (candra-śuklonnati).”
    Note: The chapter lists eight topics (bāhu, koṭi, karṇa, spaṣṭa, sita, parilekha, sūtra, kardama) covered in 18
verses. The kardama refers to the “mud” or penumbral shadow region.
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Sūtrārtha (सूत्राथर्ः):
      प                              …
    Literal Meaning: “The parilekha-sūtra itself [is] the karṇa… the parilekha-sūtra-karṇaḥ. The meaning is
clear.”
   c[0](.95.95)sanskritbg c[1](.95.95)englishbg
Hindi Bhāṣya (िहन्दी भाष्य):
Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta — Bilingual Edition — Part II, Chunk 3                                                17


     इ            ,      ,     ,       ,            ,           (     )

    English Translation: “In this way, in [the topics of] bāhu, koṭi, karṇa, spaṣṭa, sitāṅgula, parilekha-sūtra
(karṇa), by eighteen meaning-verses, is the seventh chapter named Candraśuklonnati.”
    Note: The sitāṅgula (bright digit) is a unit for measuring the illuminated portion of the Moon’s disk. One
aṅgula (digit) = 1/12 of the Moon’s diameter.

Appendix: Notes on the Text and Commentary
About the ब्राह्मस्फुटिसद्धान्त
The ब्राह्मस्फुटिसद्धान्त (Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta, “Correctly Established Doctrine of Brahma”) is the magnum opus of
the great Indian mathematician and astronomer Brahmagupta (598–668 CE). Composed in 628 CE at Bhinmal
(in present-day Rajasthan, India), it consists of 24 chapters (अध्याय) containing a total of 1008 verses (श्लोक) in
आयार् and अनुष्टुभ् metres.

Contents of This Chunk (Pages 281–420)
This typeset volume covers the following chapters from Part II of the text:

   1. चन्द्रग्रहणिवकारः — Lunar Eclipse Variations
      Deals with the computation of eclipse magnitudes, durations, and the iṣṭagrāsa (desired obscuration)
      method.

   2. सूयर्ग्रहणािधकारः — Solar Eclipse Chapter
      Covers the computation of solar eclipses, including the five jyā (sines) required: udayajyā, madhyajyā,
      ravidayākarṇa, viśimaśaṅku, and hṛkṣepa.

   3. चन्द्रच्छायािधकारः — Moon’s Shadow Chapter
      Treats the computation of the Earth’s shadow as seen on the Moon’s disk.

   4. ग्रहोदयास्तािधकारः — Planetary Rising and Setting
      Explains the methods for computing the heliacal rising (udaya) and setting (asta) of planets.

   5. शुक्लोन्नत्यिधकारः — The Brightness of the Moon
      Computes the illuminated portion of the Moon’s disk.

   6. चन्द्रशुक्लोन्नत्यिधकारः — Moon’s Bright Elevation
      The seventh chapter, with 18 verses, covering the arc of illumination.

The Commentary (नूतनितलक)
The Hindi commentary (टीका) in this edition was composed by Paṇḍita Kṛpāludatta Sūnu Sudhākara Dvivedī.
The commentary follows the traditional structure of Indian mathematical commentaries:

    • सूत्र (Sūtra) — The original Sanskrit verse

    • सूत्राथर् (Sūtrārtha) — Literal meaning of the verse (marked सु. भा.)

    • िववृित (Vivṛti) — Detailed explanation (marked िव. भा.)

    • िहन्दी भाष्य (Hindi Bhāṣya) — Hindi explanation (marked िह. भा.)

    • उपपित्त (Upapatti) — Mathematical proof or demonstration

Mathematical Notation
Brahmagupta’s work represents one of the high points of Indian mathematics. Key contributions in these chap-
ters include:

    • Rules for computing eclipse magnitudes using the भुज (bhuja, sine) and कोिट (koṭi, cosine)
                                       √
    • The कणर् (karṇa) formula: karṇa = bhuja2 + koṭi2
Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta — Bilingual Edition — Part II, Chunk 3                                        19


    • Iterative methods (असकृत् , asakṛt) for refining planetary positions

    • Proportional reasoning (त्रै रािशक, trairāśika) for time conversions

    • Geometric constructions using क्षे त्र (kṣetra, diagrams)

Algebra / बीजगिणतम् — Pages 1339–1370
[Original p. 1339–1343]
सू. भा.---शून्यिविधः । धनयोर् योगो धनम् , ऋणयोर् योगो ऋणम् ।         Sū. Bhā.—The sum of two positive quantities is positive;
धनऋणयोः योगः अन्तरं ; समे सत्य् अं शे शून्यं भवित ॥                  the sum of two negative quantities is negative. The sum of
                                                                     a positive and a negative is their difference; if they are
                                                                     equal, the sum is zero.
     [Original p. 1344–1348]
सू. भा.---शून्यगुणनम् । शून्ये न गुिणतं िकमिप शून्यं भवित । धनं      Sū. Bhā.—Zero multiplied by any quantity is zero. A
ऋणे न गुिणतं ऋणं , ऋणं ऋणे न गुिणतं धनं । शून्यं ये नािप भक्तं       positive quantity multiplied by a negative is negative; a
शून्यम् ॥                                                            negative by a negative is positive. Zero divided by any
                                                                     quantity is zero.
सू. भा.---शून्यहारः । शून्ये न िवभक्तं रािशः शून्यहारः तत् ख-बीजम्   Sū. Bhā.—A quantity divided by zero becomes that fraction
अभवत् । अस्य सं शोधनं भास्करे ण िद्वतीये न कृतम् ॥                   of which the denominator is zero. This khacheda
                                                                     (zero-divisor, or “zero-seed”) was later corrected by
                                                                     Bhāskara II.
     [Original p. 1349–1353]

अनन्तािन उत्तरािण जनियतुं शक्यन्ते ॥                                 cakravāla (cyclic) method. From this, infinite solutions can
                                                                     be generated by composition (bhāvanā).
     [Original p. 1354–1358]

अनुप्रवणतायाः, स्थलस्य च अक्षां शात् गणनीयः । चरजा                            longitude, the oblique ascension of the ecliptic, and the

समािप्तः---एषः ब्राह्मस्फुटिसद्धान्तः ब्रह्मगुप्ते न िजष्णुघाटसुतेन           Colophon.—This Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta was composed
शकाब्दे ६२८ (५५० ई.) वयिस िंत्रशे सिञ्चक्षे प । एतत् पठतां सवर्े षां          by Brahmagupta, son of Jṣṇughāṭa, in the year 628 of the

Colophon and Editorial Notes / समाप्तिटप्पणी
अस्य पाठस्य िवषयाः: ब्रह्मगुप्तकृतं गिणतज्योितषकोशम् एतत् ,            Key Mathematical Contents: The Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta
यिस्मन् धनऋणशून्यगिणतािन, कुट्टकरीितः, वगर्प्रकृितः,                   is notable for: (1) algebraic rules for arithmetic with
ग्रहस्फुटीकरणं च सिवस्तरं वर्िणतम् ॥                                   positive and negative numbers, zero, and surds; (2) the
                                                                       kuṭṭaka (pulverizer) method for linear Diophantine
                                                                       equations; (3) the varga-prakṛti method for Pell’s equation;
                                                                       (4) astronomical calculations for planetary positions,
                                                                       eclipses, and conjunctions; (5) trigonometric interpolation
                                                                       formulas.

सङ्किलतम् । ब्रह्मगुप्ते न उज्जियन्यां ६२८ ई. मिण सं किलतं             the original printed edition. Composed in 628 CE at Ujjain,
गिणतज्योितषयोः िशखरं इदं ग्रन्थकोशम् ॥                                 the work represents one of the pinnacles of Indian
                                                                       mathematical astronomy.

शङ्कुवप्रं ग्रहं भवतोित स्पष्टम्
तुल्यािदशोः


शङ्कुतलं प्राच्यपरान्तं भुजो दिक्षणोत्तरं कणर्ः
िद्वजया तिद्वपरीतमूलं िद्वक्षे पमध्यतः कोिटः
शङ्कुतलं प्राच्यपरान्तं


िद्वक्षे पसम्पातगतयोयर्थाप्राक्पूवार्परसुतयोरैं यो लम्बः स भुजः
युिक्तलक्षणम्

उभे मानक्याखिण्ड ग्रहयोमर्ध्यान्तरं युिक्तगृह्योः
समिलिप्तकयोगं हृत्वै विद्वके स्फुटमानयोगाखिण्डत्
मानक्याखिण्डके


समकलयोग्रर्हयोमर्ध्यान्तरं


सम कलात्मक दो ग्रहाें के अन्तर यिद दोनाें ग्रहाें के िवभक्तयोगाखण्ड
से न्यून हो तब ग्रह द्वय की युिक्त होती है अथार्त् ग्रहणवत् आच्छादन
(उपिरस्थ ग्रह ग्राह्य और अभःिस्थत ग्रह ग्राहक) होता है । यिद दोनाें ग्रहाें
के अन्तर स्फुट िवभक्तयोगाखण्ड से अिधक हो तब ग्रह की युिक्त नहीं
होती। िसद्धान्त शे खर में �मानक्याखिण्ड धुवरिवकरे स्यान्न्मेदोः िद्वके तू'
इत्यािद िवज्ञान भाष्य में िलिखत स्लोकां श से श्रीपित िसद्धान्त िशरोमिण
में �मानक्याखिण्ड धुवर िवकरे ऽस्ये भवे द् योगः' इत्यािद से भास्कराचायर्
ने भी आचायर्ै क के अनुरूप ही कहा है इित ॥६९॥
भग्रहत्यिधकारः
भुजकोिटकणार्नां यद्भज्ु यासाधर्ं पिरणामनाथर्ं तान् यद्भज्ु यान् व्यासाधर्हृतान्
गुणकः कुयार्त्
भुजकोिटकणार्नां यद्भज्ु यासाधर्ं पिरणामनाथर्ं तान् यद्भज्ु यान् व्यासाधर्हृतान्
गुणकः कुयार्त्

अथोपपितः । गोल्युत्थया भुजकोिटकणर्ै ःशङ्कुकुमर्स्थानवशातः स्फुटा
भुजामयर्धायां चािपहटी युिक्तराचायर्ै षां प्रितपािदता
यदा द्व्येक्षणािडकािभन्तुर्ल्याः कुत्वा तदा योगो भवे त् । योगे
ग्रहयोमर्ध्यान्तरे ष्टज्ञोिनितवत् समान्यिदशोःप्राच्यशङ्कुवप्रयोःसं योगान्तरं बाहु
कायर्वधर्न्मध्यात्तयोगवतोऽन्त-हयोः प्रत्ये कस्य शङ्कुतलस्याप्राियकं योगान्तरं
तयोभुर्जो भवत् इत्यथर्ः
ग्रहयो-रै क्ये कणार्े भवतः । स्वकणार्े भुजाकृितिवयोगाप्ते स्वकणार्े भुजयोवर्गर्योरन्तर-
मूलेकोटी भवतः । एवं ग्रहयोः कोिटभुजकणर्ै ःशङ्कुकृत् यद्भज्ु यान्
किल्पतषडिष्ट-गुणान् व्यासाधर्हृतान् िवज्याध्मातान् कृत्वाथै केन कतर्व्येन कोिटभुजकणर्ै ः
शङ्कुवो यद्भज्ु यासाधर्पिरणाता भवे तुः
प्रागपरयोःयद्भज्ु यासाधर्पिरणातपूवार्-पररे खानुल्लपयोःप्राच्योपूवर्िदिशपश्चात्
पिश्चम िदिशपृथक् कोटी प्रसायर् कोिटप्रसायार् बाहुभुजोदल्वा िदक्षु मध्यतो
मध्य िवद्धोःभुजाप्रान्तोभुजाप्रा-िवद्धले खोकणार्ं च दल्वा बाहुप्रयोःस्वभुजाप्रयोः
स्वशङ्कुमध्यात् तदप्रान्ते स्वशङ्कुवप्रयोलर्े खने यथी च पूवर्किल्पते द्वे
िद्वक्षे पमध्यस्थहच्छायामध्यिबन्दुस्थािपते न चक्षु षास्वशङ्कुवप्रं पृथक् ग्रहं
दशर्येत् । योगे ग्रहयो-योगे तयोः शङ्कुवकुव्रान्तरं भवे द्यत् शङ्कुवन्तरामावः,
शङ्कुभुजादीनां सम-त्वात् अन्यथाग्रहयुितर्िमन्ने ऽस्वरे तयोः शङ्कुवोरन्तरं
भवे देव, भ्रमे योगे शङ्कु-कुव्रान्तरसन्तरमे वान्यथा िह तयोःयोगे महायुित
शङ्कुकुव्रान्तरं ग्रहयोरन्तरं ने यं भ्रमदाग्रहयुितर्िमन्नकल्पे ऐषं ने यिमित

रिचत स्मृित-ग्रन्थ सम्मत महायुगािद में यिद ग्रहगणना करते हैं तो मे षािद
में मज्ज्ञनािद ग्रहाें की िस्थित िसद्ध नहीं होती है , श्रीपै था रिचत रोमक
में कहीं पर महायुगािद की चचार् नहीं है , इसिलये महायुग शब्द से यहाँ
आचायर् किथत महायुग ले ना चािहये , जब सब ग्रहाें को तथा पात मन्दोच्च
शीघ्रोच्चाें की िस्थित मे षािद में होती है तो उसी का नाम महायुगािद है ,
श्रीपै था किथत महायुगािद में यह िस्थित नहीं होती है इसिलये उनके मत
से महायुगािद ठीक नहीं है , आचायर्ै क यह खण्डन ठीक है

1 तृतीय जाितः — The Third Class of Partnerships
������ �: ���� �����                                                                      Chapter 1: The Third Class of Partnerships
��� ���—�������������� ������� ���� ������ ��� ����� �������������������                  Page 754—Here begins the discussion of the third class
                                                                                          of partnership problems (tṛtīya jāti), which deals with
                                                                                          combined rates of work.
   िदन सम्बिन्धपूरणकालः सवर्े षां योगः, ततोऽनुपाते नैक कालावच्छे दे न
                                                                                          Translation: “The sum of all [workers’] day-related
 मुक्तसं कुल्यािभवार्पीसं पूरणकालः, एतावदाचायार्े क्तमुपन्नम् ॥९॥ ||�||
                                                                                          filling-times is [found]; then by proportion, the time for
                                                                                          a single cistern-filling by all [working together] released
                                                                                          [at once] is found. Thus has the Ācārya stated what
                                                                                          follows.” ||9||
����:                                                                                     Commentary:
���� ���� �� ����� �� ������ �������� �� �� �� ������������ ���� �� ����� ���� �� ��      In the third class (tṛtīya jāti), savaraṇa (reduction to
���� �� ��� ��� �� ������ ������-�� �� �������� �� �� ��� ��� �� ������� �� �������       common denominators) is taught. When the numera-
�� ����� ���� �� ���� ����� ��������                                                      tor standing above is multiplied by the numerator, the
                                                                                          third-class reduction is accomplished. The denominator
                                                                                          is given to be multiplied by the denominator, and the
                                                                                          numerator standing there, being added or subtracted,
                                                                                          is multiplied—then the connection of fractions and the
                                                                                          removal of fractions is accomplished. This relates to the
                                                                                          section on partnership.

2.1 िमश्रधनानयनम् — Finding the Compound Amount
                                                                                        Translation: “The first term multiplied by the standard
            अमागकाल प्रमाणधन परकाल हीनं िद्वतीय स्थानं भवित                             period [and] divided by the other period becomes the
                                                                                        second term. The square of the other principal, added
 िमश्रघाते िमश्रकेष्ठ वगर्ं योज्यं मूलं ततोऽन्याधर्ं हीनं प्रमाणफलम् ॥१५॥
                                                                                        to the product of the mixed [amount], [and] the square
                                     ||��||
                                                                                        root [taken]; then, diminished by half of the other, is
                                                                                        the standard result.” ||15||
����:                                                                                   Commentary:
����� ��� �� ������� ���� �� ����� ����� ��� �� �� �� ���� �� �������� ����� ����,      The product of the standard period and standard prin-
���� ���� ���� �� �� ��� ���, ������ ���� ���� �� = ���� ��� �� ������ ����� ��         cipal, divided by the other period—that result is to be
������ �� ��� ������ �� ���, ����������� �� ������������ �� ���� �����                  established in two places. The result in the first place is
                                                                                        named bhrā, the result in the second place = bhranya.
                                                                                        The square of the other principal, added to the product
                                                                                        of the mixed [amount], the square root taken; from it,
                                                                                        half of the other being subtracted, the standard result
                                                                                        is obtained. ||15||

3 त्रै रािशकािदकम् — The Rule of Three and Extensions
                                                                                        Translation: “Having performed the mutual transposi-

7 क्षे त्रव्यवहारः — Plane Geometry
������ �: �����������                                                                   Chapter 7: Plane Geometry
��� ��������� ���� �������� ���������������—����������, ����������, ��������—           This major section deals with the calculation of areas
������������, �����������������, ���������, ��� ���������������� �������                of various geometric figures—triangles, quadrilaterals,
                                                                                        circles—and the relationships between their sides, diag-
                                                                                        onals, and altitudes.


7.1 समलम्बचतुभर्ुजस्य क्षे त्रफलम् — Area of an Isosceles Trapezium
                                                                                        Translation: “The mutual products of the sides and per-
                     बाहुकोट्योहर्ताः परस्परं क्षे त्रयोिरह                             pendiculars of two fields—among them, the greater is
                                                                                        the base, half is the face, and two [come from] the un-
 ते षु भूिमरिधकोऽधर्को मुखं च िवषमस्य द्वयम् ॥                              ||||
                                                                                        equal [sides].”
����:                                                                                   Commentary:
��� ���������� ��� �� ���� ���� ���� �� ���� ��, ����� ���� �� �� ��� �� ���� ��,       The diameter of the circumscribed circle of a square field
�������� �� �� ������ ��� ������� ��������� ����� ����� ������������������� �������     is its diagonal; in an oblong field also it is the same. In
����� �� ���� �� ��������� �� �� ��� �� ������� ��� �� ��� ��� �� ����������� ��� ��    the Ācārya’s text, by the word aviruddha one should
������� ���� �� ���� ����� �� ����� ������� ��� �� ���� ������ �� �� ������ (���� ��)   understand an isosceles trapezium (samlamba), where
������� �� �� ��� ������� ���� ��                                                       both diagonals and both sides are equal. Multiplying
                                                                                        the diagonal by the side and dividing by twice the al-
                                                                                        titude, the semi-diameter of the circumscribed circle of
                                                                                        the quadrilateral is obtained. In a scalene quadrilateral
                                                                                        in which a circumscribed circle is possible, the semi-
                                                                                        diameter is the square root of the product of the side
                                                                                        and the opposite side.
Brāhmasphuṭasiddhānta — Part II, Final Bilingual Edition                                                                                        10



7.2 वृत्तस्य व्यासज्ञानम् — Finding the Diameter of a Circumscribed Circle
                                                                                        Translation: “The transposed side-product of the two
            िवपयर्स्तपादं भुजयोः कणर्ं िद्वगुणावलम्बनिवभक्तः                            sides, the diagonal divided by twice the altitude—the
                                                                                        heart [semi-diameter] of that. For a quadrilateral, the
 हृदयं िद्वसस्य भुजप्रितभुजकृितयोगसूलाधर्म् ॥२६॥                          ||��||
                                                                                        semi-square-root of the sum of the products of the sides
                                                                                        and opposite sides.” ||26||
����:                                                                                   Commentary:
��� ���������������� ������� ��� ����� ������� � ��� ���� � ���� ��� ����� ���������    In a square field, the diagonal itself is clearly the diam-
������������� ������ ��� ������ �� ��� � ����������� ����� ������� ���� ���� ������     eter. By transposition, two equal sides are spoken of.
��� �������� �������������� ��� �� ��������� �������������� ������� ����                The meaning is this: the transposed side-product of the
                                                                                        sides, divided by twice the altitude—the result is the
                                                                                        heart (semi-diameter) of the circle circumscribed about
                                                                                        the quadrilateral. In a scalene quadrilateral where a
                                                                                        circumscribed circle is possible, the semi-square-root of
                                                                                        the sum of the products of the side and opposite side
                                                                                        becomes the heart (semi-diameter).


7.3 ित्रभुजोपिरगतवृत्तव्यासज्ञानाथर्म् — Circle Diameter from Triangle
                                                                                        Translation: “Now, for knowing the diameter of the cir-
               इदानीं ित्रभुजोपिरगतवृत्तस्य व्यासज्ञानाथर्माह                           cle circumscribed about a triangle, he says: the product
                                                                                        of the two sides of the triangle, divided by the altitude
         ित्रभुजस्य बाहोभुर्जयोहृर्तः कणार्े िद्वगुणावलम्बरिवभक्तः

 हृदयं िद्वसस्य भुजप्रितभुजकृितयोगसूलाधर्म् ॥२७॥                          ||��||
                                                                                        square-root of the sum of the products of the side and
                                                                                        opposite side.” ||27||
����:                                                                                   Commentary:
��� ������������ ��� �� ������ ���� ������ ������� ����������������� ��� ���� ���       In a square field, the diagonal itself is the diameter of

8 खातव्यवहारः — Excavations (Solid Geometry)
������ �: ����������                                                                  Chapter 8: Excavations (Solid Geometry)
��� ���� ��������, �������, ����������, ��� �����������������������������������       This section deals with the calculation of volumes of

8.1 समखातघनफलम् — Volume of a Regular Excavation
                                                                                      Translation: “That regular excavation of which [the
              समखातं तु यत् क्षे त्रफलं तद्वेधगुिणतं घनफलम्                           area]—the area of the field multiplied by the depth is
                                                                                      the volume. One-third of the regular excavation is the
 समखातस्य ित्रभागः सूचीखातस्य घनफलम् ॥                                        ||||
                                                                                      volume of a needle-excavation [pyramid/cone].”
����:                                                                                 Commentary:
����� �� ������ ��� ��� ���� ��� ��� ������� �� ���������� �� ������ ���� ���� ��     The area of a regular excavation is found just as the
������ �� �� (�����) ����� ����������� �� ���� ���� �� ��������� ��� ���� �� �����    area of a square or isosceles trapezium is found. The
������� �� ������ �� �������� ����� �� ���� ���������� �� ��� ����� ����������� ���   area multiplied by the depth (vedha) gives the volume of
�� ���� ���� ��                                                                       the regular excavation. That is, an excavation in which
                                                                                      the length etc. at the mouth are the same at the base
                                                                                      is called a regular excavation (sama-khāta). Dividing
                                                                                      its volume by three gives the volume of a needle-like
                                                                                      excavation (sūcī-khāta, i.e., pyramid/cone).

अस्य अिन्तमस्य खण्डस्य प्रस्तावना --- एतत् अिन्तमं भागं                    This final volume of the Brāhmasphuṭasid-

महोदयस्य अिन्तमाध्यायान् समािवशिन्त, यथाम् : गिणताध्यायस्य                 gun in the first two volumes. Pages 1677–
समािप्तः, िवकलवगार्नयनम् , कालज्ञानम् , चन्द्रग्रहणम् , मन्दस्फुटम् ,      1816 cover the concluding chapters of Brah-
शीघ्रफलम् , भोगकला, प्राणकला, च समािप्तः।                                  magupta’s masterpiece, including the comple-
                                                                           tion of the Gaṇitādhyāya, extraction of roots of

1     Conclusion of Arithmetic — Circumference & Shadow
अिस्मन् खण्डे गिणताध्यायस्य अिन्तमश्लोकाः आरभ्यन्ते , ये पिरिध-            The present section begins with the final
अनुपपित्त-छाया-व्यवहारान् प्रितपादयिन्त।                                   verses of the twelfth chapter (Gaṇitādhyāya),
                                                                           treating the calculation of circumference
                                                                           (paridhi), proportion (anupapatti), and the
                                                                           computation of shadows (chāyā vyavahāra).


1.1     Circumference and Proportion
पिरधौ क्रमशः प्राग्वत् स्वगुणात् भवे त् फलम् |                             paridhau kramaśaḥ prāgvat svaguāt bhavet pha-
श्रीपत्युक्तिमदं चाचायर्ै रनुक्रमे णावबोिधत ||१७||                         lam |
                                                                           śrīpatyuktamidaṃ    cācāryairanukrameāvabod-
                                                                           hita ||17||

(बीएसएस् १२.३७ / खण्ड २, पृ.                     ८९१) एतत् श्लोकं          (BSS 12.37 / Vol. II, p. 891) This verse states that
पिरिधः व्यासस्य उक्तगुणकैः क्रमशो गुणनया प्राप्यते इित वदित,               the circumference is obtained by multiplying
यत् पूवार्चायर्ै ः श्रीपितः-आिदिभः उपिदष्टम् । ब्रह्मगुप्त-भास्कराचायर्-   the diameter successively by the prescribed
लीलावतीिभः अिप स्वस्वग्रन्थे षु एतत् उपिदष्टम् ।                           factors, as taught by previous teachers includ-
                                                                           ing Śrīpati. Brahmagupta, Bhāskarācārya,

1.2     Mathematical Proof
अनुपपित्तः --- िमथ्यान्तः सं लग्न धान्यरािशः पिरिधवास्तव                  Upapatti (Proof): The incorrect circumfer-
पिरधे बर्ंधुतुल्यः | क्रान्तः कोण िस्थतस्य धान्यरािशः                     ence associated with a quantity of grains
पिरिधवास्तवपिरिंध चतुथार्ं शतुल्यः | बाहुकोणसं लग्न धान्यरािशः            is proportional to the true circumference.
पिरिधवास्तव पिरधे ः पादे नैक तुल्यस्ते नोत्पिरिधत्रयमानं िद्ववे द-        The inclined-angle quantity of grains is
सिचत्रभागे केन क्रमशो गुिणतां सघातं पिरिंध भवे त् | ततः पूवर्ं            one-quarter of the true circumference;
वे धफलं ततु िद्ववे द सिचत्रभागे केन क्रमशो भक्तं तदा वास्तवं तद्फलं       the base-angle quantity is one-sixth. The
भवतीत्ये तावताऽऽचायार्े क्त-मुपपन्नम् ||१७१||                             circumference- triple measure, multiplied
                                                                          successively by the Dvivedi-sacitrabhāga
                                                                          factors, yields the true circumference. The
                                                                          previous penetration result, divided succes-
                                                                          sively by the Dvivedi-sacitrabhāga factors,
                                                                          gives the true result — thus the teacher’s
                                                                          statement is proved. ||171||


1.3     End of Arithmetic Chapter
इित रािश व्यवहारः --- अब भित्त के अन्तगर्त भित्त के क्रान्तः कोणरूप       Here ends the Rāśi Vyavahāra (Arithmetic Op-
तथा बाहुकोणरूप धान्यरािश प्रमाणा-नयन के िलये कहते हैं |                   erations). Now, under division, the quantities
                                                                          of grains corresponding to the inclined angle
                                                                          and base angle are stated for the derivation of
                                                                          measures.


1.4     Shadow Calculations
छाया व्यवहार --- बीएसएस् १२.४१--४२: तज्जादौ छायात्                        BSS 12.41–42: First, for finding the time from
इष्टकालज्ञानाथर्िमष्टकालतच्छायानयनाथर्ं चाहु | छायानरसं क्रमहृतं          a given shadow, or the shadow at a given time.
शुद्धं प्रागपरयोर्िदगतदोषं | िदनगतदोषां शहृतं धु दलं छाया                 The nara (sine of latitude) multiplied by the
नरखे खं ||४२||                                                            shadow, divided by the śañku (gnomon), gives
                                                                          the śuddha (mean) value; considering the east-
                                                                          ern or western direction, this is divided by the
                                                                          dinagata-doṣ̄̄aḿsa (ascensional difference) to
                                                                          obtain the accurate shadow. ||42||

सू. भा.---नरः शङ्कुः रिभमष्टः | छायाया यो नरः शङ्कं ु भागः स              Sūtra-bhāỵa: The nara is the śañku multiplied
सं क्रमे यस्ते न                                                          by eight — the nara of the shadow divided
शुद्धं हृतं लब्धं प्राक् पिश्चमकपाले दुग्गतं पिश्चमकपाले दुग्गतं भवित |   by the śañku is that in the sañkrama; this is
                                                                          the śuddha; divided by the eastern or west-
                                                                          ern semi-diurnal arc, it becomes the corrected
                                                                          value.
छाया व्यवहारस्य िवस्तृत उपपित्तः अनुवतर्ते। नरः अक्षज्या अिस्त,           The Chāyā Vyavahāra (shadow computations)
शङ्कुः शङ्कुः (१२ अङ्गुलाः)। छाया एते षां परस्परसम्बन्धात्                section continues with detailed derivations.
गण्यते ।                                                                  The nara is the sine of latitude, and the śañku
                                                                          is the gnomon (12 añgulas). The shadow
                                                                          is computed from the relationship between
                                                                          these quantities.

1.5     Geometric Construction of Shadows
सम्बद्धिचत्रािण ज्यािमतीयसम्बन्धान् दशर्यिन्त | शङ्कुः                       The accompanying diagrams illustrate the ge-
(उन्नतदण्डः) भूतले छायां प्रकाशस्य िकरणे न सह कोणं िनमार्ित |                ometric relationships: the gnomon (vertical
                                                                             pole) casts a shadow on the ground, forming
                                                                             an angle with the sun’s ray. The karna (hy-
                                                                             potenuse) equals the distance from the lamp’s
                                                                             height. Similar triangles yield the proportions.

2      Algebraic Operations — िवकलवगार्नयन
बीएसएस् १२.४६: इदानीं सकल िवकलवगार्नयनाथर्माह |                    BSS 12.46: Now (the author) teaches the
स्विवकलपदं वगार्ं शगुणाः सकलिस्त्रशद्द्वैतो िवकलवगर्ः |            method of finding the square root of a non-
प्रक्षे प्यः सकलकृतौ वगर्धनो िद्वित्रतुल्यबधो ||४६||               square number. The square of the sakala (in-
                                                                   teger part) is subtracted from the number; the
                                                                   remainder, multiplied by the square of the de-
                                                                   nominator, becomes the vikalavarga (square
                                                                   of the non-square part). This is to be added to
                                                                   the square of the sakala, and the result gives
                                                                   the square root approximately. ||46||

(बीएसएस् १२.४६) यिद सं ख्यायाः कलाः िवकलाः च इित द्वयं             (BSS 12.46) Where a quantity has both integer
वतर्ते, तस्य वगार्थर्ं कलारािशः सकलसं ज्ञो िवकलारािशश्च स्विवकलः   (kala) and fractional (vikala) parts, the inte-
तत्कलापश्चात् शास्त्र कथये |                                       ger part is called sakala and the fractional part
                                                                   vikala; these are then processed by the rule to
                                                                   obtain the square root.


Upapatti (Mathematical Proof)

करणाथर्ं                                                           vikala parts, to find its square, the integer part
कलारािशः सकल सं ज्ञो िवकलारािशश्च स्विवकलस्तत्कलापश्चात्           is called sakala and the fractional part vikala;

(बीएसएस् १२.४७) एतत् श्लोकं िभन्नै ः सह भाजनस्य िविंध ददाित |      (BSS 12.47) This verse gives the method for di-
भाज्यं , छे दे न सह युक्तं (िभन्नयोजनिविधना), भाजकस्य अधर्े न      vision with fractions. The dividend, combined
गुिणतं , ततः प्रकृत्या युक्तं , शुद्धं लब्धं ददाित |               with the divisor (in the manner of adding frac-
                                                                   tions), is divided by half the divisor multiplied
                                                                   by the denominator of the divisor; the result,
                                                                   added to the prakṛti, gives the quotient. (BSS
                                                                   12.47 commentary)

3      Astronomical Problems — कालज्ञान
अनुवतर्मानाः खण्डाः ज्योितषीयगणनािन समािवशिन्त                     ---    The following sections deal with astronomical
कालिनणर्यम् , ग्रहिस्थितगणनम् , िविवधज्योितषीयमानािन च।                   computations, including the determination of
                                                                          time, planetary positions, and the computa-
                                                                          tion of various astronomical quantities.

4      Planetary Theory — मन्दस्फुट & शीघ्रफल
4.1      Epicyclic Model
ग्रहिस्थितगणनायां मन्दस्फुटस्य (केन्द्रसमीकरणस्य) शीघ्रफलस्य                         The computation of planetary positions in-
(िद्वतीयवक्रीभावसमीकरणस्य) च गणना आवश्यकी | ब्रह्मगुप्तः                             volves the mandasphuṭa (equation of centre)
अधोिलिखतं िविंध ददाित |                                                              and śīghraphala (equation of anomaly, or sec-
                                                                                     ond inequality). Brahmagupta gives the fol-
                                                                                     lowing method:

तद्यात् स्पष्टं ित्रकोणं तस्य स्पष्टं िद्वतीयं केन्द्रं                              eva yadi tat spaṭaṃ trikoṃ tadān̄̄iṃ viśeạṃ

5     Advanced Astronomical Computations — भोगकला & प्राणकला
इष्टज्यां सं गुणीताः पश्चकमर्णां कणर्च्चन्द्रम् |                         iṭajȳ̄aṃ sag̣ū̄itāḥ paścakarmā̄aṃ karṇac candram |
इष्टज्यापाद्युत्थ्यासवर्ं िवभािजता लब्धम् ||२५||                          iṭajȳ̄apādyutthyāsarvaṃ vibhājitā labdham ||25||
नवित छते प्रोह पदे नवते मनोरथं शे षमगणत्कला |                             navati chate proha pade navate manorathaṃ śeam-
एवं धनुिरष्टदया भवित ज्यया िवना ज्यार्िधः ||२६||                          agakalā |
                                                                          evaṃ dhanuriṭadayā bhavati jyayā vinā jyārd-
                                                                          hiḥ ||26||

(बीएसएस् २१.२५--२६) इष्टज्या पञ्चकमर्िभः सं गुिणता कणर्े न                (BSS 21.25–26) The iṭajyā (desired sine) is mul-
िवभक्ता; शे षात् नवत्यिधकात् पदात् मनोरथः (इष्टचापः) प्राप्यते |          tiplied by the results of the five operations and
एतत् ज्याधर्े ः िवना चापस्य अवलोकनम् अिस्त |                              divided by the radius; the remainder, when 90
                                                                          is subtracted from the nava position, gives the
                                                                          śeṣa; the manoratha (desired arc) is obtained.
                                                                          The arc is thus found from the sine without the
                                                                          jyārdha (half-chord).

सू. भा.---इष्ट ज्यायाः पादद्वयपिरले खेन युक्त व्यासाधर्ं तद्द्वारा ते न   Sūtra-bhāỵa: The desired sine is divided by the
िवभािजताः | शे ष स्पष्टम् |                                               semi-diameter, together with the two-foot arc-
                                                                          difference; the remainder is clear. (BSS 21.25–
                                                                          26 commentary)

(बीएसएस् २१.५०--५२) एते श्लोकाः भोगकलाः (दै िनकं चलनं चाप-         (BSS 21.50–52) These verses compute the

                                                                   tras. (BSS 21.50–52 commentary)

पदािन : (१) क्रमशः िदनयोः दीघर्त्वान्तरस्य अन्वे षणम् , (२) एतद्   in terms of kalās (arc-minutes). The computa-

                                                                      • Finding the difference in longitude be-

अब श्रिम्बज्ञु त नक्षत्र के भोगानयन और ग्रहमुक्त नक्षत्रानयन को कहते हैं ।         [Introductory Note.] Now [the Ācārya] speaks of the com-
                                                                                   putation of the motion (bhoga) of the starry nakṣatra and
                                                                                   the computation of the nakṣatra as freed from the planets
                                                                                   (graha-mukta-nakṣatrānayana).

पञ्चदशो योगभोगकलानामै क्यं = चं × १५                                               [Mathematical Rule.] The fifteenth [operation]: The sum
सवर्े षां योगः = १ चं + ३ चं + १५ चं = २७ चं = सवर्नक्षत्रभोगः                     of the degrees of conjunction-motion (yoga-bhoga-kalā) =
                                                                                   Caṃ × 15. The sum of all = 1 Caṃ + 3 Caṃ + 15 Caṃ = 27
                                                                                   Caṃ = the total motion of all the nakṣatras (sarvana-kṣatra-
                                                                                   bhogaḥ). Here Caṃ (cakra) denotes a complete revolution.

                                                                                   said] by Śrīpati in the fifteenth [chapter].
                                                                                   “The sum of the motions of all [nakṣatras] multiplied by the
                                                                                   revolutions [gives] the Vaivasvata [value]; this is the yoga
                                                                                   of the starry [nakṣatra],” and so on — thus spoke Bhāskara;
                                                                                   and all teachers [hold] this same meaning.
                                                                                   [Verses 50–51]

                                                                                     In the northern hemisphere (uttara-gola), [the latitude] be-
                                                                                     ing joined with the equinoctial shadow (viṣuvac-chāyā)
                                                                                     [in the] south (yāmya) is diminished (hīnam). Thus, the
